### Page 1

3Q recap - strong op leverage offset GPM pressure — 3Q26 net profits (+125% y/y) 
topped our/BBG estimates 35%/28% respectively owing mostly to stronger sales 
and larger degree of op leverage. Sales reached a record NT$32.4bn (+24% q/q and 
+83% y/y), 23%/12% above Citi/BBG estimate, which can be attributable to strong 
demand in edge AI, semi equipment and smart healthcare applications. GPM fell to 
36.7% (-1ppt q/q and -2.2ppt y/y) below Citi/BBG estimates of 38.0%/37.6%, 
reflecting higher component prices. Nevertheless, OPM expanded to 20.9%, 
underpinned by strong op leverage on higher sales scale. OP profits +151% y/y beat 
Citi/BBG forecast 33%/21%, the key driver to the earnings beat.    
Implications — Strong order intake is encouraging with 3Q BB ratio reaching 1.6x 
and NA particularly robust at 2.1x. However, we believe the stock's rich valuation 
limits further re-rating potential, particularly as a higher comparison base could 
moderate growth in 2027E. Continued GPM compression also reinforces our 
concerns over material/component inflation and the timing of price increases to 
offset higher costs. Advantech will host 3Q26 results call on Nov 4th. We will revisit 
our numbers and share key takeaways following management's updates.  
Neutral

Advantech
Valuation
Our target price of NT$670 is based on 35x 2H26-1H27E EPS, at the high-cycle of the stock's valuation range between 20-35x
since 2020, reflecting solid BB ratio, improving margin profile and ROE.
Risks
Key upside risks that could cause Advantech’s shares to trade above our target price include: 1) better-than-expected
marcoeconomic outlook; and 2) better-than-expected demand and margin profile. Key downside risks would include: 1)

slower-than-expected demand recovery, especially the China market; and 2) further deterioration of demand in US and EU
markets.

Guide to Citi Research Fundamental Research Investment Ratings:
Citi Research stock recommendations include an investment rating and an optional risk rating to highlight high risk stocks.

Risk rating takes into account both price volatility and fundamental criteria. Stocks will either have no risk rating or a High risk
rating assigned.
Investment Ratings: Citi Research investment ratings are Buy, Neutral and Sell. Our ratings are a function of analyst
expectations of expected total return ("ETR") and risk. ETR is the sum of the forecast price appreciation (or depreciation) plus
the dividend yield for a stock within the next 12 months.  The target price is based on a 12 month time horizon. The Investment

rating definitions are: Buy (1) ETR of 15% or more or 25% or more for High risk stocks; and Sell (3) for negative ETR. Any covered
stock not assigned a Buy or a Sell is a Neutral (2). For stocks rated Neutral (2), if an analyst believes that there are insufficient
valuation drivers and/or investment catalysts to derive a positive or negative investment view, they may elect with the approval
of Citi Research management not to assign a target price and, thus, not derive an ETR. Citi Research may suspend its rating

and target price and assign “Rating Suspended” status for regulatory and/or internal policy reasons. Citi Research may also
suspend its rating and target price and assign "Under Review" status for other exceptional circumstances (e.g. lack of
information critical to the analyst's thesis, trading suspension) affecting the company and/or trading in the company's
securities. In both such situations, the rating and target price will show as “—“ and “-“ respectively in the rating history price

chart. Prior to 11 April 2022 Citi Research assigned “Under Review” status to both situations and prior to 11 Nov 2020 only in
exceptional circumstances. As soon as practically possible, the analyst will publish a note re-establishing a rating and
investment thesis. Investment ratings are determined by the ranges described above at the time of initiation of coverage, a
change in investment and/or risk rating, or a change in target price (subject to limited management discretion). At times, the

expected total returns may fall outside of these ranges because of market price movements and/or other short-term volatility
or trading patterns. Such interim deviations will be permitted but will become subject to review by Research Management.
Your decision to buy or sell a security should be based upon your personal investment objectives and should be made only
after evaluating the stock's expected performance and risk.

call to indicate the analyst expects the share price to rise (fall) in absolute terms over a specified period of 30 or 90 days in
reaction to one or more specific near-term catalysts or events impacting the company or the market. A Catalyst Watch will be
published when Analyst confidence is high that an impact to share price will occur; it will be a STV when confidence level is
moderate. A Catalyst Watch or STV Upside/Downside call will automatically expire at the end of the specified 30/90 day

period. The Catalyst Watch will also be automatically removed if share price performance (calculated at market close) exceeds
15% against the direction of the call (unless over-ridden by the analyst). The analyst may also remove a Catalyst Watch or STV
call prior to the end of the specified period in a published research note. A Catalyst Watch/STV Upside or Downside call may be
different from and does not affect a stock’s fundamental equity rating, which reflects a longer-term total absolute return

our ratings distribution table for our Catalyst Watch/STV Upside/Downside rating system. However, we reiterate that we do
not consider No View to be a recommendation. For all Catalyst Watch/STV Upside/Downside calls, risk exists that the
catalyst(s) and associated share-price movement will not materialize as expected.

### Page 1

MediaTek

TPU wins on track for steep ramp through 
’28 joined by further CSP/AI lab potential

Reiterate Rating: BUY | PO: 6,200 TWD | Price: 3,915 TWD

3Q26 beat on consumer builds; 4Q26 lifted by TPU ramp 
3Q26 sales were +10% QoQ on its flagship mobile SoC launch and customers’ pre-builds 
ahead of its chipset price hike on rising manufacturing cost. On higher sales and better 
mix, GM/OpM edged up to 46%/16%, taking 3Q26 EPS to NT$17.5 above BofAe/street. 
4Q26 sales was guided +25-32% QoQ, in line with BofA preview, as steep TPUv8t ramp 
now tracking to US$2.5bn (vs. US$2bn guide before) partially balanced by soft demand 
elsewhere (-15-22% QoQ vs. 5-year seasonal +1%) following the early pull-in. Despite 
TPUv8t growing to ~35% of its sales at lower margins, GMs is guided resiliently at 45% 
in 4Q26, implying better margins for its merchant business at 47%. Fast sales scale-up 
and stable GMs should lift operating leverage with OpMs guided for 19% at midpoint. 
ASIC pipeline spanning across CSPs and frontier AI labs 
Mediatek sees TPUv8t ramping to US$16bn+ revenue in ’27, lifting OpM to 20%+, with 
upside if it could secure more capacity. The follow on TPUv9 is tracking for a meaningful 
production potential in ’28. Importantly, it confirmed a 2nd ASIC customer win in design 
now and production contribution set to kick off in ’28. We believe this new project would 
require Mediatek’s component sourcing and supply chain management at a much higher 
dollar content. This along with potential Bytedance win could add upside to our base 
case for its data center revenue of US$19bn/US$53bn in '27/'28 with xAI ASIC revenue 
joined from 2H28 to supplement Google TPU upgrades.  
AI project diversification supports earnings and valuation 
As ASIC business inflects, we lift our 2026 EPS to NT$74.13 and keep ‘27/28 EPS at 
NT$168.93/404.32. Keep our Buy rating and NT$6,200 PO on 24x 2H27-1H28E P/E 
(implied 30x ASIC EPS + 10x merchant EPS), backed by sales/EPS 90%/134% CAGR. 
Growing AI project wins should alleviate the concerns on customer concentration and 
TPU competition debate with share downside protected by 23x/10x ‘27/28 straight P/E.

Company Sector 
Semiconductors

Company Description 
MediaTek is a fabless IC design company supplying to 
wireless communications and digital consumer markets. 
Before taking leadership in mobile SoC area, it had built 
solid ground in the TV SoC and optical disk drive markets. 
The firm invests heavily in R&D to seek better positioning 
in the 5G cycle, and has a complete offering including ASIC, 
Wi-Fi, PMIC etc. to address applications beyond 
smartphone, such as IoT and auto.

Investment Rationale 
We have Buy rating on MediaTek as we think MediaTek is 
ready and has the strength to brace for the upcoming 
proliferation of IoT devices and opportunities in growing 
markets like ASIC, ARM-based PC, auto, and etc., and we 
believe its solid balance sheet and cashflow could provide 
downside support to the share price. Meanwhile, we expect 
5G smartphone penetration to be a multi-year theme for 
the company, with its better competitive landscape in 
duopoly 5G era and an improved margin profile.

### Page 4

Price objective basis & risk 
MediaTek (MDTKF) 
Our PO of NT$6,200 is based on 24x 2H27-1H28 P/E. which is towards the higher end 
of its historical P/E range of 8-25x, as we believe the company's broadening ASIC 
pipeline could drive an earnings upgrade cycle.

Upside risks to our PO are: (1) better than expected smartphone demand, especially in 
China, (2) a better pricing in mobile communication segment thanks to alleviated 
competition and rising exposure to high-end/flagship market, and (3) an even more 
superior investment return on non-op side to uplift its net cash position. (4) Unexpected 
collaboration with global technology leaders leads to higher than expected 
margins/profits

Downside risks to our PO are: (1) weaker-than-expected end-market situation in 
consumer-related smartphone/TV space, (2) macro challenges including inflation/policy-
related pressures, (3) rising competition from existing players and/or newcomers in 
smartphone/TV SoC (system on chip) space, and (4) ASP/margin pressure caused by each 
or all of the above and/or rising foundry costs. (4) Bidding process for Meta Inference 
ASIC is slower than expected.

2027 price hike should be case by case amid push back from some customers:
Management said 2027 pricing negotiations could be mixed across customers even
though capacity is nearly fully loaded. Some need more capacity in 4Q26 and are
willing to pay the new post-hike wafer price. However, the company still faces push
back from some customers, likely tied to their inventories. Our recent checks
suggest  Tier 1 foundry L TA wafer pricing into 2027 could be less than 10%, below

the buyside bogey of a 25-30% blended hike. However, for some tier 2 customers,
the price hike could indeed be around 25-30%. We believe the average like-to-like
L TA wafer price could only increase by  15-20% in 2027. Stay EW.
SOI demand exceeds expectation: The company said 12-inch SOI demand already
exceeds its current expansion plan. The priority is to debottleneck and ramp existing
Missouri capacity. More than half of the newly installed tools have qualified for

volume production, and RF SOI and silicon-photonics products have entered volume
production.
4Q26 could be relatively muted: We expect 4Q26 to be relatively muted since
wafer pricing remains tied to older agreements, existing capacity is fully loaded, and
new capacity depreciation would continue to weigh on margin.

Micron/GWC deal marks a new milestone for GWC
Micron Technology and GlobalWafers announced a deeper strategic partnership on July 9,

2026, centered on a 10-year supply agreement and US$500mn of strategic financing from
Micron to support GlobalWafers' new 12-inch silicon wafer facility in Sherman, Texas. On
its latest earnings call, GWC board proposed executing that financing as up to US$500
million of 10-year privately placed convertible bonds, with Micron as the sole subscriber,
carrying a 0.5% coupon. The agreement secures significant raw silicon wafer capacity for
Micron's long-term manufacturing plans, with the financing linked to the buildout of

GlobalWafers America's (GWA) Sherman facility.
GlobalWafers Chairwoman Doris Hsu described the agreement as the largest and longest
contract in the company's history. The two companies will also collaborate on next-
generation wafer technologies and process innovation. The Sherman plant is notable as
the only US-based source of advanced 12-inch wafers.
The agreement forms part of Micron's broader US$3bn commitment to strengthen the US
semiconductor supply chain and accompanies its target of manufacturing 40% of its

DRAM output domestically by the mid-2030s. We believe GWC is well positioned to

### Page 4

M
Idea
4
support ongoing foundry and memory capacity expansion in the US, not only for Micron
but also for TSMC and Samsung, which are already GWC's two largest customers. We
currently assume Micron/GWC L TA pricing could be US$70-80 on average for the polished
wafer (10-20% above current L TA pricing after discounts), reflecting the higher US cost
base. This would be broadly in line with, or slightly above, L TAs signed in 2021-22.
Current inventory levels remain higher than during the 2021-22 upcycle

when existing L TAs were signed
Notably, customer inventories of 12-inch wafers remain well above levels seen during the
previous industry upcycle. According to SUMCO's inventory trend data (see Exhibit 3 ),
inventory volumes and turnover days were near the lowest levels of the 2014-26 period
during 2021-22, reflecting a lean and highly utilized supply chain. By contrast, the current
cycle presents the opposite picture: shipment volumes are approaching record highs, but

customer inventories are also near record levels, with inventory days and absolute
inventory exceeding those seen even at the peak of the 2021-22 upcycle.
This backdrop differs meaningfully from the 2021-22 environment and, in our view,
supports a more moderate and delayed pricing recovery in 2027 rather than a repeat of
the broad-based increases seen during the prior cycle. In 2021-22, wafer makers held
significant pricing leverage as customer inventories sat near cycle lows despite record

shipment volumes. Fabs consumed nearly all purchased wafers with minimal inventory
buffers, creating a tight supply environment that enabled wafer manufacturers to
implement meaningful price increases across 12-inch wafers.
While shipment volumes currently remain elevated, customers also hold substantial
inventory. This provides a buffer against demand growth and moderate supply disruptions,
reducing the urgency to secure incremental wafer supply. Historically, this dynamic

weakens suppliers' pricing power. Until inventories normalize meaningfully, wafer makers
are likely to have less pricing leverage than they did entering the 2021-22 upcycle. Our
view is that 2027 L TA negotiations will focus primarily on removing current discounts
rather than driving nominal price increases. We broadly expect 2027 industry L TA pricing
increases of 15-20% in average, with Tier 2 customers' wafer price to be raised 25-30% but
Tier 1 customers would only see 5-15%.

Micron Technology Inc. (MU.O)

in AI, in-line with broader semis.
Risks to Upside
n Customers continue to demonstrate an appetite to take on inventory around macroeco-
nomic uncertainty
n Additional wafer intensity of HBM further improves overall supply and demand
n MIcron's HBM share surpasses expectations
Risks to Downside

n Pricing can turn quickly; a falter in end demand with inventories elevated could lead to
a swift price reduction
n HBM demand falters and competition intensifies, pressuring pricing

STOCK RATINGS
Morgan Stanley uses a relative rating system using terms such as Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated or Underweight (see definitions below). Morgan Stanley does not assign ratings of Buy,
Hold or Sell to the stocks we cover. Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated and Underweight are not the equivalent of buy, hold and sell.  Investors should carefully read the definitions of all
ratings used in Morgan Stanley Research. In addition, since Morgan Stanley Research contains more complete information concerning the analyst's views, investors should carefully read Morgan

Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone.  In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice.  An investor's decision
to buy or sell a stock should depend on individual circumstances (such as the investor's existing holdings) and other considerations.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

기아 (000270)

26.3Q Review: 수익성에 대한 입증이 필요  Company Brief

26.3Q Review: 매출액 31.6조원, 영업이익 1.8조원  
기아는 26.3Q 매출액 31.6조원 (+10.1% YoY, -4.4% QoQ), 영업이익 1.8조원 
(+24.3% YoY, -30.8% QoQ, +5.8% OPM) 등의 실적을 기록했다. 26.3Q 영업이익은 
시장 컨센서스 (2.36조원)를 22.9% 하회했다 [iM증권 추정치: 매출액 31.5조원, 
영업이익 2.3조원]. 주요 구성 요소별 내용은 아래와 같다. 
① P: 26.3Q 26.3Q 전동화 차량 판매는 30.0만대 (+47% YoY, 판매 비중 
36.2%)를 기록했는데, 이 중 HEV 17.2 만대 (+46% YoY), EV 12.3 만대 
(+75% YoY)로 구성됨. 미국 HEV 판매가 전년 동기 대비 118% 증가하며 
시장 성장률을 약 4배 상회했고, 특히 서유럽 EV 판매가 전년 대비 163% 
증가함. 단 26.3Q 중 원화가 빠르게 강세로 전환되며 원/달러, 원/유로 
평균 환율은 전분기 대비 각 5.2%, 6.2% 하락함에 따른 – 효과 관찰됨. 
② Q: 26.3Q 도매 판매는 84.7만대 (+7.9% YoY) 를 기록했고, 북미, 유럽, 
인도 등 주요 시장에서 고른 성장 나타남. 26.3Q 소매 판매 또한 82.9만대 
(+6.9% YoY)로 글로벌 산업 수요 (-6% YoY)를 상회. 북미는 HEV, 내수와 
유럽은 EV, 인도는 신형 셀토스와 소넷을 중심으로 판매가 확대됨 (이하 
26.3Q 권역별 도매 판매 YoY, 국내: +2.2%, 북미: +7.6%. 유럽: +19.3%. 
인도: +38.5%). 이란 사태 장기화로 아중동 도매 판매는 전년 동기 대비 
28.4% 감소했으나, 수요가 견조한 중남미와 CIS로 물량을 전환하며 
감소분을 상당 부분 만회함. 연간 도매 판매 가이던스 (335만대)를 유지함. 
③ C: 26.3Q 관세 비용은 약 0.7조원으로 추정되며, 전년 동기 대비 
5,000억원 감소함 (25.2Q 0.7조원, 25.3Q 1.2조원, 25.4Q 1.0조원, 26.1Q 
0.8조원). 반면 인센티브 증가 및 가격 인하 7,270억원, 서유럽 EV 비중 
확대에 따른 믹스 악화 4,650억원, 원자재 가격 상승 영향 3,680억원 등 
총 1.6조원의 감익 요인이 발생함. 이에 26.3Q 매출원가율은 84.1%로 
전년 동기 대비 3.0%p 상승함 (인센티브 +1.9%p, 재료비 +1.2%p). 다만 
26.3Q 기말 환율 급락하며 판매보증충당부채 환입 규모가 축소되었고 
판매보증비 약 9,000억원 감소. 판매관리비율은 10.1%로 3.7%p 개선됨. 
26.3Q 기아 순이익은 1.29조원으로 전년 동기 대비 9.6% 감소했다. 원화 강세에 
따라 외환환산 관련 이익이 전년 동기 540억원 이익에서 3,718억원으로 손실 
전환됐고, 현대모비스 등 관계사 이익 감소로 지분법손익 또한 360억원 감소했기 
때문이다 (현대차 순이익 감소 논리와 동일). 1) 산업 수요 감소와 중동 판매 차질에도 
권역별 파워트레인 대응을 통해 그룹사 대비 가지고 있는 외형 성장성을 증명했으나, 
2) 유럽 EV 판매 확대 과정에서 발생한 인센티브와 믹스 악화라는 비용 부담, 원자재 
가격 상승이 관세 감소 효과를 대부분 반납했고, 3) 분기 중 급격한 원화 강세가 
더해지며 시장 기대를 크게 하회하는 수익성을 기록했다는 것이 26.3Q 실적 총평이다. 
Buy (Maintain) 
목표주가(12M) 150,000원(하향) 
종가(2026.10.30) 95,600원 
상승여력 56.9 %

실적 발표 후 주가 급락: 성장에도 불구하고 수익성에 대한 우려  
26.10.30 실적 발표 당일 기아 주가는 11.7% 하락했다. 하루 전 발표된 그룹사 실적 
발표와 상반된 모습이었다. 시장이 이번 실적에서 확인한 우려는 아래와 같다. 첫째, 
성장과 이익의 괴리다. 관세 영향을 제외한 26.3Q 영업이익은 2.52조원으로 전년 
동기(2.70조원) 대비 오히려 1,770억원 감소했다. 판매 증가에 따른 물량 효과 
(+4,420억원)와 환율 효과 (+5,290억원)가 발생했음에도, 인센티브와 믹스 악화로 이를 
대부분 반납한 것이다. 기아의 외형 성장 상당 부분이 유럽 EV 판매 증가에서 나오는데, 
해당 시장에서는 중국 EV 업체와의 경쟁으로 공격적인 프로모션이 불가피하다. 26.3Q 
유럽 권역에서의 중소형 EV 판매가 사업 계획을 크게 웃돌며 EV 믹스는 13.1%에서 
14.8%로 확대됐다. 시장은 이를 M/S를 위해 수익성을 훼손하고 있다고 받아들이는 
것이다. 둘째, 당장 26.4Q에도 유의미한 수익성 개선이 없을 것이라는 점이다. 기아는 
26.3Q 컨퍼런스 콜에서 4Q 영업이익률이 3Q 수준과 크게 다르지 않을 것이며, 연초 
제시한 연간 가이던스 (영업이익 10.2조원, +8.3% OPM) 에 다소 미달할 것이라고 
밝혔다. 중동 분쟁의 영향이 최소 연말까지 이어지고, 원자재와 물류비 부담 또한 
지속될 것이 회사의 판단이다. 무엇보다 원/달러 환율이 26.2Q 평균 약 1,505원에서 
26.3Q 약 1,430원, 현재 1,320~1,340원 수준까지 하락하며 이에 따른 - 영향이 커질 
수 있다. 셋째, 그룹사와의 이익 체력 비교다. 현대차는 26.3Q 파업과 생산 차질로 도매 
판매가 11.3% 감소했음에도 원가 절감을 통해 영업이익이 5.5% 증가했고, 시장 
기대를 상회했다. 반면 기아는 판매가 7.9% 증가했음에도 영업이익이 컨센서스를 20% 
이상 하회했다. 26.3Q 양사 영업이익 격차는 약 8,600억원까지 벌어진 상황이다.  
단기 노이즈를 제외한다면 기아의 기업 가치는 여전히 높다  
그럼에도 당사는 기아의 EV 전략, 낮은 수익성을 이유로 중장기 기업 가치에 대한 
기대를 낮출 이유는 없다고 판단한다. 첫째, 외부 요인을 걷어내면 본원적인 수익 
창출력은 유지되고 있다. 기아는 26.3Q 영업이익률을 낮춘 외부 요인을 원자재 1.2%p, 
중동 판매 차질 0.7%p, 환율 1.7%p 등 총 3.6%p로 분석했다. 즉 중동 분쟁이 
해소되면 상당 부분이 사라질 일회성 요인이다. 둘째, EV 수익성 개선 경로는 HEV가 
걸어온 길과 같다. 기아는 HEV 역시 도입 초기에는 수익을 내지 못했으나 현재는 ICE에 
버금가는 수익성을 내고 있다며, 26~27 년 현재의 EV 전략을 유지하겠다고 밝혔다. 
유럽 ICE 시장은 26.3Q 전년 동기 대비 약 20% 축소된 반면 EV 시장은 50% 이상 
성장하고 있다. 전환기에 스케일을 확보하지 못하면 EV 마진을 개선할 방법도 없다. 
기아는 향후 3년간 배터리, 모터, 히트펌프 등 주요 부품에서 연 10% 수준의 원가 
절감을 추진하고, 2~3년 내 EV를 HEV와 같은 수익성 경로에 올려놓겠다는 계획이다. 
셋째, 중장기 성장 계획을 수정하지 않았다. 기아는 27년 사업 계획이 CID 당시 제시한 
숫자와 큰 차이가 없을 것이며, 중장기 영업이익률 10% 목표도 유지했다. IAA 시행에 
대비해 슬로바키아 공장에서 EV를 전량 생산하고 ICE 국내 수출 방안도 검토 중이다. 
주주환원 측면에서도 TSR 35% 를 지키고, 자사주 매입보다 배당을 우선하겠다는 
입장이다. 10/30 종가와 최소 DPS 기준만으로도 배당수익률은 6.3%에 달하고 있다.

기아 판매관리비 (좌) 판매관리비율 (우)
  26.4Q 기아 매출액 31.8조원, 영업이익 2.2조원 전망 
기아 26.4Q 실적을 매출액 31.8조원 (+13.2% YoY), 영업이익 2.2조원 (+20.8% YoY, 
+7.0% OPM)으로 전망한다. 26.4Q 판매 성장세는 이어지고, 3Q에 집중 반영된 원자재 
부담 일부 완화와 계절적 성수기 효과로 3Q 대비 수익성은 소폭 개선될 것이다. 다만 
1) 원화 강세에 따른 평균 환율 하락, 2) 유럽 EV 인센티브의 높은 수준 유지 (미국은 
할부 금리 상승으로 QoQ 약 200달러 증가, 유럽은 전분기와 유사), 3) 중동 판매 
차질과 원자재, 물류비 부담 지속을 감안하면 의미 있는 반등은 어렵다. 이는 4Q 
영업이익률이 3Q 수준과 크게 다르지 않을 것이라는 회사의 언급과도 궤를 같이한다. 
기아 목표주가 150,000원으로 하향  
기아 목표주가를 26.4Q 추정치 하향을 반영해 150,000원으로 하향한다. 기아의 판매 
경쟁력은 금번 분기에도 증명됐다. 다만 EV 판매 확대가 수익성 개선으로 연결된다는 
점이 숫자로 확인되기 전까지, 시장은 기아의 성장에 할인율을 적용할 것이다. 중동 
분쟁 해소와 EV 원가 절감이 맞물리는 27년이 기아가 수익성을 증명할 시점이 될 
것이며, 6%를 상회하는 배당수익률이 그때까지 주가의 하방을 지지할 것으로 판단된다.

 회사는 해당 종목을 1%이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다.  
 금융투자분석사와 그 배우자는 해당 기업의 주식을 보유하고 있지 않습니다.  
 당 보고서는 기관투자가 및 제 3자에게 E-mail등을 통하여 사전에 배포된 사실이 없습니다.  
 회사는 6개월간 해당 기업의 유가증권 발행과 관련 주관사로 참여하지 않았습니다. 
 당 보고서에 게재된 내용들은 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하고 있으며, 외부의 부당한 압력이나 간섭 없이 작성되었음을 확인합니다. 
본 분석자료는 투자자의 증권투자를 돕기 위한 참고자료이며, 따라서, 본 자료에 의한 투자자의 투자결과에 대해 어떠한 목적의 증빙자료로도 사용될 수 없으며, 어떠한 경우에도 작성자 및 
당사의 허가 없이 전재, 복사 또는 대여될 수 없습니다. 무단전재 등으로 인한 분쟁발생시 법적 책임이 있음을 주지하시기 바랍니다.

현대차 (005380)

26.3Q Review: 이제 올라가야 할 때 Company Brief

본업 Review: 26.3Q 매출액 45.1조원, 영업이익 2.6조원  
현대차는 26.3Q 매출액 45.1원 (-3.4% YoY, -8.3% QoQ), 영업이익 2.6조원 (+5.5% 
YoY, -6.1% QoQ , +5.9% OPM ) 등의 실적을 시현했다 [iM증권 추정치 매출액: 
45.3조원, 영업이익: 2.4조원]. 매출액은 시장 컨센서스에 부합했고, 영업이익의 경우 
시장 컨센서스였던 2.4조원을 소폭 상회했다. 주요 구성 요소 별 내용은 아래와 같다.  
① P: 26.3Q HEV 판매 대수는 16.6만대를 기록하며 전사 판매 대비 비중 
18.2% 달성 (+18.8% YoY, -11.1% QoQ). 미국의 경우 국내 공장 생산 
감소에 따른 수출 부진으로 도매 판매는 소폭 감소했으나, 판매 비중은 
25.2%로 전반적인 믹스는 직전 분기와 유사. 26.3Q 평균환율이 직전 분기 
대비 크게 하락했으나, 전년 동기 기저 탓에 오히려 + 환율 효과 관찰됨. 
② Q: 26.3Q 도매 판매 대수는 91.8만대로 전년 동기 대비 11.5% 감소 (이하 
YoY 기준, 미국: -10.0%, 유럽: -18.1% 국내: -27.2% 인도: + 18.3%). 
26.2Q 안전 공업 화재에 이어 26.3Q에 발생한 파업의 영향으로 생산 
대수는 감소. 국내 공장에서의 생산 차질은 내수 판매에 더해 수출 감소, 즉 
전체적인 판매 축소로 연결됨. 26.4Q부터 생산 관련 노이즈는 발생하지 
않을 전망. 이에 더해 아반떼, 투싼 등의 신차 판매 효과를 통한 반등 가능.  
③ C: 26.3Q 관세 비용은 직전 분기와 유사한 수준으로 발생한 것으로 파악 
(25.2Q 0.8조원, 25.3Q 1.8조원, 25.4Q 1.5조원, 26.1Q 0.8조원, 26.2Q 
0.9조원). 이란 사태에 따른 주요 원자재 가격 상승 압력은 계속 존재하나, 
내부 원가 절감 정책으로 약 5,000억원의 원가 상승분을 상쇄. 현대차는 
26.4Q 원가 절감 정책의 효과가 더 크게 나타날 것이라고 언급. 26.3Q 
기말 환율이 전년 동기 대비 급락함에 따라 판매보층충당부채전입액 또한 
감소 (26.3Q 매출원가, 판매관리비 각 전년 동기 대비 1.7%, 19.2% 감소). 
금융 부문의 경우 26.3Q 매출액 9.1조원 (+27.8% YoY) , 영업이익 4,710억원 (-
18.2% YoY, -37.9%) 등의 실적을 기록했다. 완성차 판매가 전반적으로 
위축되었음에도 외형 성장을 이어갔으나, 금리 상승에 따른 이자 비용 등 전반적인 
조달 관련 비용이 증가했다. 일부 EV를 중심으로 중고차 가격이 소폭 하락한 점 또한 
HCA 등 주요 부문 이익 감소의 배경이다. 26.3Q 현대차 순이익은 1,932억원을 
기록했는데, 이는 영업외손익이 전년 동기 789억원에서 229억원으로 감소했기 
때문이다. 현대차는 주요 환율 하락에 따른 외화환산손실 증가의 영향이라고 밝혔다. 
1) 생산 단에서의 노이즈가 연속해 발생하며 내수 판매 및 수출 감소가 불가피했으나, 
2) 원자재 가격 상승 영향을 내부 원가 절감 정책을 통해 상쇄했고, 3) 주요 
평균환율이 하락했으나 기말환율 또한 급락하며 판매보증충당부채전입액 감소 효과가 
나타나 시장 기대를 소폭 상회하는 수익성이 관찰되었다는 것이 26.3Q 실적 총평이다. 
현대차는 2026년 도매 판매 가이던스를 416만대에서 400만대로 하향 조정했다. 
26.3Q 누적 도매 판매 대수는 288만대이기에 하향 조정은 불가피했다. 단 신규 
가이던스에 따르면 26.4Q 판매 목표는 약 110만대 수준이라는 것인데, 아반떼, 투싼, 
주요 모델 페이스리프트, GV80 HEV 등의 신차 효과가 얼마나 나타날지가 관건일 
것이다 (이하 분기 도매 판매, 25.1Q 100.1만대, 25.2Q 105.7만대, 25.3Q 103.8만대).

### Page 2

현대차 (005380)
2
미래 사업 업데이트: 자율주행은 계획대로, Atlas는 조금씩 변신중
26.3Q 컨퍼런스 콜에서 특별히 SDV~Boston Dynamics 관련 업데이트는 확인되지
않았으나 여러 매체를 통해 파악된 현대차그룹의 미래 사업 진행 상황은 다음과 같다.
① SDV/자율주행: 데이터 플라이 휠에 대한 중요성 강조. 27년 말부터 광주
권역 자율주행 실증 사업에 투입되는 차량은 Atria AI 활용. SDV 플랫폼이
적용된 차량 양산 시작은 29년. 양산 SDV 출하량 증가함에 따라 29년부터
GPU 5 만개 이상으로 구성되는 새만금 데이터센터를 자체 운영을 목표함.
박민우 포티투닷 사장 겸 AVP 본부장이 부임한 이후 Tesla, nVIDIA 출신의
개발 인력을 충원 중인 상황. 26.05 그랜저 페이스리프트 모델에 플레오스
탑재 시작. 26.09부터 판매되고 있는 신형 아반떼에도 플레오스 탑재 완료.

② Boston Dynamics: 10.8 Rohit Prasad를 CEO로 선임. Rohit Prasad은
아마존에서 Alex 개발에 중추적인 역할을 수행했던 인물. 현대차그룹에서
직접 하드웨어 개발 및 양산을 챙기고, 소프트웨어에 역량을 높이겠다는
행보로 판단. 26.10 Atlas의 신규 덱스트러스를 공개했는데, 기존 제품 대비
자유도가 크게 증가 (7DoF→14DoF). 순수 직구동 방식을 여전히
고수함.10.27 Boston Dynamics가 주요 부품 업체에게 Atlas 1만대 물량의
부품 PO를 곧 발송할 것이라는 기사가 보도됨. 28년까지 연 3만대 CAPA의
공장 완공이라는 기존 계획 대비 양산 및 개발 속도가 빨라진다는 시그널임.
현대차그룹의 미래 사업 업데이트를 또 찾아볼 수 있을 가장 빠른 이벤트는 CES
2027이다. 다만 CES 2027에는 현대차가 아닌 그룹사가 참여함에 따라 해당 행사에서

유의미한 업데이트 내용은 찾아보기 힘들 것이며 이에 중장기 관점의 접근이 필요하다.
시장의 우려가 반영된 주가 하락: 빨라질 속도와 올라올 본업에 주목
현대차 주가는 26.2H 접어들며 크게 하락하고 있고, CES 2026 전후의 상승폭을 사실상
모두 반납했다 (연초 기준 -1.3%, 26.2H 이후 -41.7%). 또한 최근 시장 반등
흐름에서도 사실상 소외되고 있다. 현대차를 바라보는 투자자들의 우려는 아래와 같다.
첫째, CID 전후로 Boston Dynamics 관련 업데이트가 너무 부족하다는 것이다. 26.08
현대차 CID 행사를 앞두고 많은 투자자들은 빅테크 업체들의 지분 투자 및 Atlas
개발과 관련해 새로운 내용을 기대한 것이 사실이다. 이런 상황에서 유럽, 내수 등 주요
지역 에서의 판매 업황 또한 좋지 않다 보니, 시장의 실망은 더욱 컸다. 전술한 것과
같이 CES 2027에 현대차가 참여하지 않는다는 점에서 내년에 대한 기대도 크지 않다.

둘째, 내수와 유럽 업황이 그룹사 대비 너무 좋지 못하다는 점이다. 26.3Q 현대차 내수,
유럽 도매 판매는 전년 동기 대비 각 27.2%, 18.1% 감소했다. 반면 26.3Q 기아 내수,
유럽 도매 판매는 전년 동기 대비 각 2.2%, 18.7% 증가했다. 특히 유럽에서의 부진은
EV 라인업이 상대적으로 빈약하고, 중국 업체와의 경쟁이 심화되고 있다는 구조적
문제에서 출발하기에, 투자자들은 26년을 넘어 27, 28년의 성장까지 우려하고 있다
단 우리는 Boston D ynamics의 Atlas는 양산이라는 방향성이 설정된지 오래되지
않았다는 점을 기억해야 한다. 초기 단계부터 양산을 위해 개발된 Tesla Optimus 대비
개발 속도가 빠를 수는 없다. 1~2만대 수준의 데모 제품이 확보되고, 이를 바탕으로
데이터 수집이 일정 부분 확보되는 이후부터 관련 사업 확대 속도는 빨라 질것이다.

취약 지역 판매 업황의 경우에도 내수를 시작으로 점진적으로 개선될 것이다. 생산 차질
외에도 Tesla 및 중국 OEM들의 시장 침투가 우려됨은 분명하지만, 주력 차종 중에서도
국내 판매 볼륨이 큰 아반떼와 투싼의 신차 효과는 분명 기대되는 지점이다. 유럽에서의
반등은 시간이 필요할 것이나, 일단 아이오닉 3가 26.4Q부터 본격적으로 판매될
예정이다. 단 이는 큰 변동 없이 IAA가 2028년부터 시행된다는 전제에 근거하고 있다.
현대차 목표주가 600,000원 유지: 여전한 Multiple 할증 요인
현대차 목표주가를 600,000원으로 유지한다. 26년 EPS 기준 6~8배 수준의 P/E
Multiple을 부여받고 있는 Peer Group들은 중국 시장 M/S 축소로 수익성이 급락하고
있고, 미래 사업 경쟁력은 여전히 전무하다. 현대차의 Multiple 할증 요인은 충분하다.

그림16. IAA 법안 개요

자료: iM증권 리서치본부

그림17. 유럽 생산 OEM 생산원가 추이 
 `  
자료: iM증권 리서치본부

IAA는 EU판 IRA로 볼 수 있음. 현대차그룹의 경우 현대차 (터키, 체코)와 기아 (슬로바키아) 모두 유렵 현지
생산 라인을 이미 확보했고, 현대모비스를 중심으로 한 부품 자급률 비중 이미 높음. 또한 주요 부품에 있어
FTA 체결국의 원산지를 EU산과 동일하게 적용하는 규정 존재. 실제 발효까지 큰 변수가 없다면 국내 완성차
업체의 경우 구조적으로 수혜 클 것. 단 중국 업체들에 대한 명시적 배제 규정이 없다는 점은 한계 중 하나. 
IAA 주요 조항 주요 내용
적용 대상
공공조달 차량, 공적 지원(구매 보조금 등)을 받는 차량, 법인차가 대상임. 전기
차, PHEV, 수소차에 적용됨. 2028년부터 회원국은 무공해 및 저공해이면서 EU
산인 차량이 아니면 법인차를 지원할 수 없음. 판매 금지가 아니라 수요 측 혜택
을 역내 생산과 연동하는 구조
역내 부품 요건
1단계(발효 6개월 후)는 EU 조립, 배터리 제외 부품가치 70% EU산, 셀 포함
배터리 핵심부품 3개 EU산을 요구함. 2단계(1단계 3년 후)는 배터리 핵심부품
5개(셀, 양극재,BMS 포함)와 전동 파워트레인·전장 가치 50% EU산이 추가됨.
소형차 특례
전장 4.2m 이하 전기 승용차가 대상임. EU 조립에 더해 부품가치 70%와
배터리 핵심부품 3개 중 하나만 충족하면 됨. 충족 시 CO2 규제상 1.3대로 인정
됨(2025~34년).
FDI 조건 1억 유로 초과 투자가 대상이며, 지분 49% 상한, EU 인력 50% 이상, EU산 투 입재 30% 이상 등의 조건이 붙음.
FT A·관세동맹국 예외
FT A나 관세동맹을 맺은 국가의 부품은 EU산과 동등한 부품으로 인정되며,
정부조달협정(GPA) 가입국도 일부 조항에 포함됨. 단 최종 조립국까지 인정되
는지는 불명확하며, 조문상으로는 EU, EEA 역내 조립만 인정되는 것으로 해석
됨. 따라서 한국산 부품은 EU산으로 인정 가능하나, 한국이나 튀르키예에서 조
립한 완성차는 요건 미충족 가능성이 높음.
FT A 예외 배제 조건
집행위는 위임법으로 특정국을 동등 대상에서 제외할 수 있음. 해당국이 EU 기업
에 내국민 대우를 하지 않는 경우, 공급망 의존도나 공급 안보 위협이 있는 경우,
해당 협정의 예외 조항에 해당하는 경우가 배제 사유임. 중국은 FT A가 없어 애초
에 대상이 아님.
2020 2021 2022 2023
대당 생산원가 30,683 32,029 35,079 38,140
대당 판매가격 34,404 27,557 31,244 33,560
1인당 인건비 70,981 74,904 78,348 88,198
통상적으로 중국 OEM의 생산 원가 (중국 생산 가정)은 글로벌 OEM 대비 30% 낮다고 추정됨. BYD의 경우 돌핀 등과 같
은 C 세그먼트 차량에서의 유럽 수익성이 낮지만, A~B 세그먼트 급의 돌핀 서프의 경우 매우 높은 수익성 시현. 이는 유럽
시장 내의 A~B 세그먼트 경쟁 강도가 낮다는 점에서 기인. 즉 100% 수준의 관세가 부과되지 않는다면 BYD의 가격 경쟁
력은 유지되는 것. 따라서 EU는 2,000유로 수준의 중국 OEM의 대당 이익을 상쇄하기 위해 이에 준하는 수준으로 지급되
는 EV 구매 보조금 및 법인 차량 세제혜택을 검토 중인 것이고, 이를 IAA에 담아낼지가 핵심.
유럽 생산 OEM 생산원가 및 판매가격 추이

그림23. 26.3Q 국내 완성차 실적 Summary

자료: iM증권 리서치본부

현대차 미주: 펠리세이드 리콜 이슈 존재하나, 메타플랜트 및 기존 앨러배마 공
장 HEV 생산을 통한 시장 진출 속도 빨라질 것으로 전망
인도: GM 탈레가온 공장 인수를 통한 생산량 확대에 나설 예정이고, 이
를 통한 인도 내수 수요 대응 및 글로벌 신흥 시장 진출 또한 목표
유럽: 기아 대비 EV 라인업의 폭에 열위에 있으나, 캐스퍼 EV, EV 3를
통해 EV 수요 회복 국면을 앞두고 있는 유럽 시장 대응에 나설 예정
기아 미주: 텔룰라이드 HEV 출시를 통해 Mid-Size  HEV 수요 대응 이뤄지
고 있음. 스포티지 및 셀토스 등 HEV를 통한 기존 차급 대응 강화 전망
인도: 2030년까지 10개 차종 출시 목표하고 있고, 이를 통해 2030년
41만대 도매 판매, 시장 점유율 7.6% 달성 가이던스를 제시함
유럽: EV2 출시를 통한 EV 라인업 완성도 높임. 소형 해치백 차량과 신
규 HEV 차량 출시, 그리고 PBV 판매 본격화를 통한 초과 성장 목표

당 보고서 공표일 기준으로 해당 기업과 관련하여, 
 회사는 해당 종목을 1%이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다.  
 금융투자분석사와 그 배우자는 해당 기업의 주식을 보유하고 있지 않습니다.  
 당 보고서는 기관투자가 및 제 3자에게 E-mail등을 통하여 사전에 배포된 사실이 없습니다.  
 회사는 6개월간 해당 기업의 유가증권 발행과 관련 주관사로 참여하지 않았습니다. 
 당 보고서에 게재된 내용들은 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하고 있으며, 외부의 부당한 압력이나 간섭 없이 작성되었음을 확인합니다. 
본 분석자료는 투자자의 증권투자를 돕기 위한 참고자료이며, 따라서, 본 자료에 의한 투자자의 투자결과에 대해 어떠한 목적의 증빙자료로도 사용될 수 없으며, 어떠한 경우에도 작성자 및 
당사의 허가 없이 전재, 복사 또는 대여될 수 없습니다. 무단전재 등으로 인한 분쟁발생시 법적 책임이 있음을 주지하시기 바랍니다.

종목추천 투자등급

3Q26: 그래도 믿는다  Company Brief

3분기 매출 6조 943억원/영업이익 4,520억원 
한화에어로스페이스의 3분기 매출은 6조 943억원/영업이익 4,520억원(OPM 7.4%)로 
컨센서스와 당사 추정치를 모두 대폭 하회하는 실적을 기록하였다. 앞서 실적을 발표한 
한화오션의 실적 쇼크를 감안하더라도, 주력사업인 지상방산 부문의 실적이 매출 1조 
2,455억원/영업이익 217억원(1.7%)로 지난 분기(2조 1,075억원/ 5,330억원/ 25.3%) 
는 물론 전년동기(2조 1,098억원/5,725억원/27.1%)보다도 현저히 감소하였다. 
일회성 요인은 대전사업장 폭발사고에 따른 복구비용 등 300억원, 일부 국내사업 
충당금 160억원 및 환율하락에 따른 효과 등이 있었으나 역시 이를 감안해도 내수와 
수출 모두 실질 이익률은 5%미만으로 추정된다. 지상방산 분기 매출은 1분기(1조 
2,211억)보다 오히려 소폭 증가하였으나 영업이익이 1분기 2,087억원에서 
217억원으로 대폭 감소한 점에 대하여 회사는 프로젝트별 믹스 구성에 따른 낮은 
이익률의 인도분이 반영되었다고 설명하고 있으나, 그럼에도 불구하고 수출과 내수 
이익률 모두 BEP에 가까웠다는 점은 쉽게 이해가 가지는 않는다. 다만 방산기업의 
특성상 대전사업장 사고에 따른 실제 납기 영향 등 회사가 공개할 수 없는 사정이 
있었을 것으로 추측할 뿐이다. 대전사업장은 현재 복구작업이 진행 중이며 27년 
상반기 중에는 가동 정상화할 것으로 예상된다.  
긍정적인 소식도 있다. 폴란드향 천무의 연간 인도대수가 기존 가이던스인 40대에서 
60대로 증가하였으며, 이를 포함하여 3분기에 인도되지 않은 물량의 대부분이 
4분기에 집중될 예정으로, 올해의 4분기 실적 개선폭은 통상적으로 볼 수 있는 
4분기의 계절적 집중도보다 더 커질 것으로 예상한다. 방산기업의 실적은 인도일정에 
따라 크게 변할 수 있는 분기 실적보다 연간 합산이 더 중요하다는 점을 한 번 더 
믿고, 4분기를 기다리는 게 좋겠다.

투자의견 BUY 유지, 목표주가 1,200,000원 하향  
투자의견 BUY를 유지하지만, SOTP기반 목표주가는 기존 1,490,000원에서 
1,200,000원으로 19.5% 하향한다. 비영업가치 중 자회사 한화시스템과 한화오션, 
쎄트렉아이의 지분가치 하락(합산 8.7조원→7.1조원), 그리고 지난 분기 대비 늘어난 
별도 순차입금(3.25조→4.95조원)을 반영하였으며 최근 방산섹터 전반의 밸류 하락을 
감안하여 영업가치 중 지상방산+항공우주의 Target P/E를 기존 35배에서 30배로 
조정하였다. 
3분기는 여러모로 악재가 겹쳤다. 주식시장 전반의 하락에 더해 방산섹터 수주 지연에 
따른 디레이팅, 그리고 납기 지연에 따른 실적 하락 등 나올 수 있는 악재는 다 
나왔다고도 볼 수 있다. 그러나 뒤집어보면 지연중인 수주는 시간의 문제일 뿐 가시화 
가능성이 매우 높으며, 실적 또한 진행률 인식이 아닌 인도기준 인식에 따른 일시적 
현상으로 4분기 이후 복구될 가능성이 크다. 펀더멘털의 유의미한 변화는 없다고 볼 
수 있으며 밸류에이션 매력도가 증가한 현 시점에서 추가 매수를 고려할 때다. 다만, 
대전사업장 사고에 따른 당국의 제재조치가 아직 확정되지 않았음은 잔여 리스크 
요인이다. 동종사의 신규계약 입찰제한 제재조치가 주가에 상당한 영향을 미쳤음을 
고려하면 한화에어로스페이스 또한 제재조치 여부를 지켜본 후 투자할 것을 권한다.

Compliance notice 
당 보고서 공표일 기준으로 해당 기업과 관련하여, 
 회사는 해당 종목을 1%이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다.  
 금융투자분석사와 그 배우자는 해당 기업의 주식을 보유하고 있지 않습니다.  
 당 보고서는 기관투자가 및 제 3자에게 E-mail등을 통하여 사전에 배포된 사실이 없습니다.  
 회사는 6개월간 해당 기업의 유가증권 발행과 관련 주관사로 참여하지 않았습니다. 
 당 보고서에 게재된 내용들은 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하고 있으며, 외부의 부당한 압력이나 간섭 없이 작성되었음을 확인합니다. 
 당 연구원은 한화에어로스페이스(012450)의 일부 비용을 지원받아 한화에어로스페이스 공장 투어(2026년 10월 1-2)를 다녀왔습니다. 
본 분석자료는 투자자의 증권투자를 돕기 위한 참고자료이며, 따라서, 본 자료에 의한 투자자의 투자결과에 대해 어떠한 목적의 증빙자료로도 사용될 수 없으며, 어떠한 경우에도 작성자 및 
당사의 허가 없이 전재, 복사 또는 대여될 수 없습니다. 무단전재 등으로 인한 분쟁발생시 법적 책임이 있음을 주지하시기 바랍니다.

[투자의견] 
종목추천 투자등급  
종목투자의견은 향후 12개월간 추천일 종가대비 해당종목의 예상 목표수익률을 의미함.  
 Buy(매수): 추천일 종가대비 +15% 이상  
 Hold(보유): 추천일 종가대비 -15% ~ 15% 내외 등락 
 Sell(매도): 추천일 종가대비 -15% 이상 
산업추천 투자등급

 Overweight(비중확대) 
 Neutral(중립) 
 Underweight(비중축소)

제약업종

◼ 3Q26 파업 영향에도 컨센서스 상회 
◼ 4Q26 지연 물량 인식 및 신규 공장 기여로 실적 개선 전망 
◼ 투자의견 매수, 목표주가 2,000,000원 유지

투자의견 매수, 목표주가 2,000,000원 유지 
목표주가는 DCF 방식으로 산출(WACC 8.1%, 영구성장률 3.5% 적용). 기
존 공장의 높은 가동률을 기반으로 5공장 매출 기여 확대, 미국 생산거점 
확보, 신규 모달리티 진출을 통한 중장기 성장 지속 전망.

3Q26 Review: 파업 영향에도 컨센서스 상회  
3Q26 매출액 1조 4,903억원(+18.5% yoy, +12.8% qoq), 영업이익 6,401
억원(+1.1% yoy, +9.1% qoq), 영업이익률 42.9%(-7.5%p yoy)를 기록하며 
당사 추정치 및 시장 컨센서스 상회. 5월 1차 파업에 따른 매출 차질(약 
1,500억원) 에도 불구하고, 기존 1~4공장 Full 가동이 지속되며 견조한 실
적 달성. 5공장 PPQ 배치 생산 확대와 및 미국 록빌 공장 매출 인식도 외
형 성장에 기여. 다만, 신규 생산시설 초기 가동비와 제품 믹스 영향으로 
영업이익률은 전년 동기 대비 하락.

4Q26 매출액 1조 5,764억원(+22.6% yoy), 영업이익 7,075억원
(+34.0% yoy) 전망  
4Q26에는 2분기 파업으로 지연된 생산물량의 순차적인 매출 인식과 미국 
록빌 공장 매출 기여, 5공장 PPQ 배치 생산 확대로 실적 개선 예상, 동사
는 신규 생산시설의 매출 가시성 확보를 바탕으로 2026년 매출 성장률 
20%를 무난히 달성할 것으로 전망(연초 가이던스 15~20%).

신규 수주 회복과 포트폴리오 확장에 주목 
미국 의약품 관세정책 불확실성 완화로 지연됐던 수주 협상이 재개되며 신
규 수주 환경 개선 중. 3Q26 약 3억달러의 신규 수주를 확보했으며, 대형 
계약 협상도 구체화되고 있어 2027년 수주 회복 기대. 이는 5공장 가동률 
확대와 중장기 성장 가시성을 높이는 요인. 6공장은 ‘26년말~’27년초 착
공을 목표로 기존 증설 일정 유지. 
폴리펩타이드 그룹 인수는 1차 공개매수 응모율 87.88%를 기록하며 연내 
인수 완료 추진 중. 인수 완료 시 펩타이드 CDMO로 사업영역이 확대되며 
글로벌 생산거점 및 고객 기반 다변화 기대.

Document 277: AI泡沫破裂了吗？高盛：当下更像1998年，而非2000年

Source PDF: all report pdf/AI泡沫破裂了吗？高盛：当下更像1998年，而非2000年.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-11-07
Pages: 5
Status: ok

### Page 1

⾼盛交易台表⽰，当下这波抛售更像 1998 年流动性危机，⽽⾮ 2000 年泡沫破裂，是杠杆强
制出清⽽⾮商业逻辑被证伪。市场剧烈重定价的是建设融资与时间节奏，⽽⾮底层需求。
此外，市场⽬前最关注的电⼒与许可问题恰恰是最不重要的⼀条，供给受限并不等于需求
消失。真正需要回答的问题是：收⼊端能否最终证明所投⼊资本的合理性。
摘要：
科技股遭遇剧烈抛售，但究竟是杠杆出清还是商业模式被证伪，这决定了截然相反的操作⽅
向。
据追⻛交易台，⾼盛 FICC 与股票交易台在 11 ⽉ 6 ⽇报告中表⽰，纳斯达克指数单⽇下跌 5.5% ，
费城半导体指数重挫 12% ，标普 500 指数下跌 4.4% 。⾼盛现⾦股票业务数据显⽰，当⽇美国现
⾦股票业务净卖出 30 亿美元，其中信息技术与通信服务板块占 22 亿美元，科技板块累计净卖出
规模已扩⼤⾄ 270 亿美元，超过 7 ⽉那轮 210 亿美元的技术性抛售。与此同时， VVIX 单⽇变动幅
度为 2018 年 2 ⽉ 5 ⽇ " 波动率末⽇ " 以来最⼤。

⾼盛 FICC 与股票业务负责⼈ Rich Privorotsky 在上述⽇更中明确表态，倾向于将本轮下跌定性为
类似 1998 年的流动性危机，⽽⾮ 2000 年科技泡沫破裂的前兆。他的核⼼判断是：AI 增⻓引擎本
⾝可能依然完好，市场剧烈重定价的是建设融资与时间节奏，⽽⾮底层需求。这⼀判断对投资
者的操作含义⾄关重要 —— 若类⽐成⽴，历史上此类插曲最终都演变为买⼊机会。
杠杆⾃我强化，⽽⾮估值重估
本轮抛售的机制特征指向强制去杠杆，⽽⾮基本⾯驱动的估值重估。
Privorotsky 指出，杠杆 ETF 在收盘前的强制平仓放⼤了半导体与纳斯达克的跌幅，叠加做市商负
gamma 敞⼝形成⾃我强化的清算循环。从资⾦流向看，⾼盛主经纪业务数据显⽰，半导体板块
的核⼼压⼒在于⻓线机构（ LO ）基础需求的缺席，多策略基⾦、量化基⾦、⻓线机构与散⼾呈
现同向卖出， " 所有⼈站在同⼀边 " 本⾝即是问题所在。
相关性上升、动量因⼦跑输、残余杠杆因⼦持续抛售，⽽低⻉塔、防御与质量资产获得追捧
—— 这⼀组合读数与典型的强制降⻛险特征⾼度吻合。从选举后两⽇的市场演变来看，第⼀⽇ AI

股跑输⽽周期股因政治中⼼化预期反弹，第⼆⽇则是剧烈的补跌：若数据中⼼建设⼤幅放缓，
名义增⻓预期将⾯临下修，周期股的反弹本⾝便存在重新定价的压⼒。
1998 还是 2000 ：两种模板，两种结局
Privorotsky 将本轮下跌与两个历史节点并排⽐较，最终倾向于 1998 年模板。
⽀持 1998 年类⽐的理由在于：当年俄罗斯违约与⻓期资本管理公司（ LTCM ）危机是外部冲击，
但美国经济增⻓本⾝保持完好；⼀旦流动性恢复、美联储转向宽松，市场迅速收复失地。对应
到当下， AI 增⻓逻辑可能同样完好，市场重定价的是建设融资与推进节奏。
此外，当前科技领导者拥有真实收⼊、现⾦流与资产负债表，这与 2000 年泡沫时期的公司有本
质区别；推迟数据中⼼建设并不消灭算⼒需求，受限产能反⽽可能改善存量资产的经济性。
与 2000 年最相似的不适感在于⽀出规模的庞⼤以及回报的不确定性。核⼼问题是：市场正在清
算⼀个健全的增⻓故事，还是正在发现其经济逻辑本⾝存在缺陷？ Privorotsky 倾向于前者，但

⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⻅闻历 搜索

### Page 2

强调需求、利⽤率与回报数据必须保持稳固。
AI 建设四类担忧，市场抓错了重点
⾼盛梳理了⽀撑本轮抛售的四类市场担忧：⼀是资本不⾜以⽀撑建设规模；⼆是收⼊不⾜以证
明投资合理；三是电⼒与许可容量不⾜以⽀持算⼒扩张；四是总体⽀出轨迹不可持续。
Privorotsky 认为，市场⽬前最关注的是第三条 —— 电⼒与许可问题，但这恰恰是四类担忧中最
不重要的⼀条。供给受限并不等于需求消失。真正需要回答的问题是：收⼊端能否最终证明所
投⼊资本的合理性。
在政治变量⽅⾯，得州候任州⻓ Gina Hinojosa 的表态⽐市场解读的更为温和。她明确表⽰： " 我
们可以实现经济发展，同时保护得克萨斯州⺠众，这两者并不互斥 " ，并重申 " 我并⾮憎恨数据中
⼼ " 。 Privorotsky 认为，这更像是监管层⾯的重置，⽽⾮意识形态上的拒绝，实际⽀出或将好于
当前市场折价所隐含的预期。
技术位与买点前提
从技术⾯看，纳斯达克已跌破 200 ⽇均线，标普 500 则刚好守在该均线上⽅，这⼀⽀撑位能否守
住⾄关重要。负 gamma 环境意味着市场在任⼀⽅向均存在剧烈波动的空间。
Privorotsky 在质量偏好上明确点名台积电与阿斯⻨，认为在当前不确定性下应向⾼质量科技资
产倾斜。
他同时列出改变市场叙事所需的三项条件：AI 投资形成政治正反馈、融资市场企稳，以及底层
⽀出与需求保持完好的实证证据。反证条件同样清晰：若 AI 资本开⽀遭到实质性推迟且收⼊端证
伪投资逻辑， 1998 年类⽐失效，需转向 2000 年模板；若融资市场率先出现再融资失败，下跌性
质将发⽣根本改变。
从更⻓周期看， Privorotsky 援引历史数据指出， 1950 年以来 19 次中期选举后 12 个⽉，标普 500
全部录得上涨 —— 但他强调，这是 12 个⽉维度的观察，⽽⾮⼀个⽉的预测，短期择时依然困
难。能源⽅⾯，油价单⽇反弹约 4% ，市场此前对伊朗局势快速缓和的定价过于乐观，谈判尚未
解决，能源冲击对利率、增⻓预期与⻛险偏好的复杂化影响仍是稳定化进程的重要变量。

中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。

Document 297: Blue Wave_ Democrats sweep the US midterms

Source PDF: all report pdf/Blue Wave_ Democrats sweep the US midterms.pdf
Report source: 渣打銀行 (Market Watch)
Date: 2026-11-05
Pages: 10
Status: ok

### Page 1

Blue Wave: Democrats 
sweep the US midterms  
Summary 
Event: The Democrats gained control of the House of 
Representatives and the Senate at the US midterm elections 
that took place this week.  
Our View: With the US government now no longer under 
unified control of the Republicans, gridlock has returned to 
Washington. We believe investors should not overreact to 
these results and should stay invested. 
In equities, investors should  continue to diversify their 
equity exposure into more defensive sectors.  As we 
transition to mid -cycle for the equity markets , we expect 
stocks to continue to deliver positive returns in the 8 -10% 
range per annum, a downshift from the torrid pace of gains in 
the earlier part of the cycle.  
In bonds, income remains attractive given recent spread 
widening. We favour non -hyperscaler investment grade 
corporate bonds at the three to seven -year part of the 
curve. We continue to expect the US dollar to remain range-
bound in the near  term, as we expect two more rate hikes 
from the Fed, but as this mini rate hike cycle ends in the 
second quarter of 2027, the USD index (DXY) is likely to 
depreciate towards 98.5 as markets start to anticipate a Fed 
rate cut in the second half of next year.  
Oil is likely to rebound in the short -term on heightened 
geopolitical risk, but as supply chains adapt to the current 
situation and Congress exercises oversight on defense 
spending, WTI prices are likely to drift towards our 12-month 
target of USD 80 per barrel.

Democrats made solid gains across the Senate, House 
of Representatives and State Governors 
Results reported as at 7:45PM Singapore Time

Source: The Wall Street Journal, Standard Chartered

### Page 2

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 5 November 2026

Major implications of Democrat control of 
Congress   
1) Fiscal expansionary policies may slow. Democrats are 
now in position to block any new tax cuts or spending plans 
from the Trump administration. At the same time, any fiscal 
policies proposed by Democrats may also face Trump’s veto. 
Hence the fiscal trajectory of the US will likely remain stuck  
over the next two years. As we get into the second half of 
2027, the US debt ceiling will once again loom, and gridlock 
could potentially bring back government shutdowns. 
However, as they have always done in the past, compromise 
will ultimately prevail, in our opinion.  
2) Trump remains in control of foreign policy.  With the 
midterms now out of the way, could Trump decide to re-start 
combat operations with Iran? We believe there is a risk that 
US and Israel – now that both countries’ have concluded 
important elections – will once again be tempted to restart 
combat operations to force an end to the conflict. However, 
the duration of any renewed hostilities may be limited by 
Congress next year as the Democrats will likely withhold 
funding for further military operations. In the near -term, oil 
prices may experience renewed volatility before stabilising at 
a lower level next year. The introduction of new tariffs on 
trading partners may prove harder with Democrat oversight. 
3) AI data centre moratoriums could grow, though model 
development regulatory changes would be harder to 
pass. While the headlines have focused on the House and 
Senate results, one important development has also 
occurred at the gubernatorial level. Notably, Democrats have 
won the governor races in Texas and Georgia, two big data 
centre states. As data centre build out permits are largely 
decided at the local state level, this will have important 
ramifications for build out plans next year.  However, any 
Democratic proposals to regulate AI model developments 
would need to be signed off by President Trump, who 
continues to wield veto power on any such proposals.   
Asset class strategy 
Equities: Remain invested and continue to diversify .  
While investors initially overreacted, President Trump’s 
relatively conciliatory response has provided some welcome 
relief, helping markets recover strongly from their pre-market 
lows. Hence, we recommend investors avoid 
overreacting, remain invested and continue to diversify 
their equity exposure across sectors and regions.  
A few themes stand out from the early pre-market reaction to 
the mid-term elections. On the positive side, defensive stocks 
continue to fare well, while themes favoured by Democrats 
are also outperforming. Clean energy is a notable 
beneficiary, with solar s tocks among the biggest winners

Policy uncertainty and a change in the innovation cycle 
is consistent with our view of a mid-cycle transition, 
where equity returns moderate but remain positive

focused investors, we would recommend staying within the

2 of 8 October 19, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MACRO STRATEGY
The U.S. Capex Boom No One is Talking About—And Its Implications
Joseph Quinlan, Managing Director and Head of Market Strategy
Ariana Chiu, Assistant Vice President and Investment Strategist
We have long been of the belief that the U.S. is on the cusp of an inbound FDI capex boom
that can result in numerous positive spillover effects for the U.S. economy. The latest

figures from the U.S. Department of Commerce bear this out.
Exhibit 1 highlights the growing push among foreign multinationals to be inside the U.S.,
with FDI totaling nearly $240B in the first six months of this year. That is a staggering
103% jump from a year ago and places FDI inflows on track for a record year.
The latest inbound capex already adds to the massive scale of foreign investment in the
U.S. To wit, the accumulated stock of FDI in the U.S. stands at nearly $6T, based on the

latest figures from the Bureau of Economic Analysis (BEA). Manufacturing accounts for
roughly 43% of the total, and, at last count, some 10,000 foreign affiliates were operating
in the U.S.
These firms are largely invisible to the average U.S. investor, but the activities of U.S.
affiliates of foreign multinationals are substantial and competitive- and growth-enhancing.
To this point and based on the latest data from the BEA, foreign affiliates in the U.S.

employed nearly 8.6 million U.S. workers in 2024. That represents 6.1% of total private
sector employment, with manufacturing and retail the two most prominent sectors
of employment. Roughly 24% of all manufacturing jobs in the U.S. are from foreign
subsidiaries. In terms of pay, U.S. workers at these firms make $89,000 annually, 7% higher
than the national average, according to the Global Business Alliance.
Foreign affiliates also contributed some $1.5T to U.S. GDP; comprised some 15.7% of total

U.S. private sector investment; accounted for 12.4% of total U.S. research & development
(R&D) expenditures; paid roughly 20% of all U.S. federal taxes; and made up 22.5% of
U.S. exports and 26.2% of U.S. imports. 1 These are the direct effects of U.S. subsidiaries
of foreign companies operating in the U.S. The indirect effects are even greater in terms
of more income, jobs, growth and tax revenue—all bolstering the long-term competitive
advantage of the U.S. In a nutshell, foreign firms operating in the U.S. are an important

ingredient of U.S. economic growth.
There are multiple factors behind surging inflows: One, due to rising U.S. trade
protectionism, foreign multinationals increasingly want to be “inside” the U.S. economy,
not outside. Companies that once served the U.S. market exclusively through exports
increasingly face higher costs and more uncertainties and hence want to be “inside.”
Two, with a $32T-plus economy with extraordinary consumer purchasing power, foreign

firms prefer to be closer to their customers via investment than just trade (exports). There
is no more dynamic consumer in the world than the American shopper.
Three, America’s massive investment cycle in AI, data centers, semiconductors, power
generation, etc., has created numerous investment opportunities for many foreign firms
that don’t want to miss out on America’s surging capex cycle.
Four, and finally, abundant energy supplies, deep capital markets and a highly developed

technological ecosystem have also induced more FDI inflows.
As more U.S. affiliates of foreign multinationals set up shop in the U.S., think more job
creation across the country, more manufacturing output, more locally sourced purchases,
more U.S. exports, more spending on R&D, and more tax revenue for local communities.
And over the long run, think a more globally competitive U.S. economy.
1 Activities of U.S. Affiliates of Foreign Multinational Enterprises, 2024” and “Activities of U.S. Affiliates of Foreign

Multinational Enterprises, 2023”, BEA July 2026.
Investment Implications
The boom in foreign investment in
the U.S. should continue to support
employment, productivity, corporate
earnings, and broader U.S. economic
competitiveness, in our view. Hence
our U.S. bias.

### Page 3

3 of 8 October 19, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
And speaking of global competitiveness, Exhibit 2A is very illustrative of the magnet pull of
the U.S. economy to attract FDI versus the rest of the world. Of new greenfield investment
this decade, note the overriding preference for the U.S., accounting for nearly 16% of total
global greenfield investment this decade. America’s share is larger than the next three
countries combined—the United Kingdom, India, and Germany. And note that America’s

share of new investment is more than 5X that of China’s. Most of this new investment
has gone into manufacturing sectors like electrical equipment, computers, appliances and
batteries and has originated from Asia and Europe.
The bulk of foreign investment comes from Europe, followed by Japan and Canada, but as
Exhibit 2B highlights, there is a new dynamic playing out when it comes to America’s ability
to attract capital. There is a new source in town: a handful of developing nations (namely

Taiwan, South Korea, Mexico and Brazil). Mexican manufacturers, Taiwan technology
leaders, South Korea chip leaders, Brazil industrial firms, India pharmaceutical firms—these
companies and more are realizing that in today’s trade environment, the best way to serve
the American market is not necessarily through exports but through investment—investing
locally. FDI flows from developing nations reached $63B in the 12 months through June,
more than double the level a year prior ($24.5B), according to the BEA. Fueled by insatiable

AI demand, flows from South Korea alone totaled $23.4B in the first half of 2026. The
upshot is that an increasingly important source of capital for the U.S. is coming from an
unlikely source: the developing nations.
More fire for the capex boom. The coming boom in FDI inflows is supportive of our thesis
that the U.S. stands on the cusp of a major industrial upgrade. We are in the early innings
of a structural—not cyclical—capex cycle that should lead to the rebuilding of domestic

supply chains, higher value-added manufacturing, and more AI-driven productivity growth.
Foreign firms, in our opinion, will be part of this process.
That is important to recognize because remember the following: when a foreign company
builds a factory or an R&D facility or expands its existing manufacturing capacity right
here in the U.S., good things happen. Demand for U.S. goods and services goes up (as does
demand for construction, materials, logistics and professional services). New jobs are created,

boosting incomes of U.S. workers, while supporting more local spending and consumption.
And because many foreign firms often bring advanced manufacturing technologies when
investing in the U.S., the end results include a boost in productivity and innovation in various
sectors of the economy.
All the above, of course, are the secret ingredients to stronger U.S. economic growth, rising
corporate earnings and higher equity prices. With a little help from our friends, America’s

industrial renewal is gaining strength and momentum.

MARKET VIEW
Europe’s Electoral Cycle and Market Risks
Ehiwario Efeyini, Director and Senior Investment Strategist
As midterm elections approach for the U.S. next month, voters across Europe will also
enter their own election season starting this November and continuing throughout
2027. Over the next year, 52% of the total EU population, representing 46% of EU GDP
across eight countries, will go to the polls, including voters in three of its four largest

economies (Exhibit 3). Having announced snap general elections earlier this month,
Spain will vote for members of its lower and upper house on November 29. A series of
parliamentary elections are then due for 2027 in Estonia (by March 7), Finland (on April
18), Greece (by July 25), Slovakia (by September 28), Poland (by November 11) and Italy
(by December 22). And perhaps most consequentially France will stage Presidential
elections on April 18, with a potential second round of voting set for May 2.

For the EU as a whole, the significance of these coming elections will be the potential
for a shift toward more populist, domestically oriented policy priorities from leading
opposition candidates (particularly in France, Spain and Poland) which could obstruct the
process of closer EU integration. In the nearer term, adoption of the new seven-year
(2028-2034) EU budget due by the end of 2027 could be impeded. And over the longer
term, renewed efforts toward an EU-wide capital market union to deepen and attract

more inward investment into European markets could also be further delayed.
At the country level, investors have seen bond yields rise globally in 2026 on the back of
higher inflation and concerns over fiscal sustainability. The debt question has arguably
been most pressing in parts of Europe, especially given the likelihood that upcoming
elections make for domestic increases in government largesse. And of the eight
countries going to the polls over the next year, France remains the biggest source of

concern. Interest rate spreads on French 10-year sovereign bonds have overtaken those
in both Greece and Italy this year (Exhibit 4), driven by investor concerns over public
debt. The 2027 government budget unveiled earlier this month projected a deficit of
5.0% of GDP (well in excess of the 3% specified in the EU Stability and Growth Pact),
but remained insufficiently expansive to nullify the risk that the current government
could be unseated later this year if it is forced through the fragmented parliament. This

comes on top of an existing debt load of close to 120% of GDP and a halving of official
2026 growth forecasts from 1.0% at the start of the year to 0.5% in September. In
short, all four variables in France’s debt sustainability equation currently stand as major
headwinds, and the two leading candidates in the 2027 election have shown limited
appetite for fiscal consolidation over the coming years.
Exhibit 3: Europe's Upcoming Elections Represent Around Half of EU GDP and Population.

Investment Implications
We remain cautious on European
markets overall, despite seeing
pockets of strength in selected
areas such as defense, data center
infrastructure and renewable energy.
On top of growing uncertainty
from the upcoming electoral cycle

and interest rate outlook, rising
competition and slowing demand
from China continue to challenge
key segments of the Consumer
Discretionary sector. And limited
direct AI concentration also leaves
Europe under-exposed to the key
growth theme that continues to buoy
equity markets in the U.S., Japan and
emerging Asia.

Intervention from the ECB under its 2022 bond purchase program is also unlikely at this

stage given that the spread widening in France has not appeared either disorderly or
unwarranted. Meanwhile, conditions could potentially be aggravated by expected ECB rate
hikes in response to still above-target inflation across the eurozone. Indeed recent moves
to tighten monetary policy from a number of major central banks have been read most
hawkishly in Europe. Most of the increase in European bond yields has come from higher
inflation breakevens as opposed to real yields in the U.S. and Japan. And despite some

paring back of tightening expectations in the wake of recent bond volatility, the market is
still pricing three more policy rate increases over the next 12 months.
Euro depreciation and an emerging shift toward more protectionist trade policy with China
could further complicate the ECB policy outlook. China posted a record $35B trade surplus,
according to Eurostat, with the EU in September (alongside its $938B total surplus for the
first three quarters of 2026), and the two markets have agreed to reduce the EU bilateral

deficit by capping imports of hybrid cars for four years. Hybrids account for less than 2%
of total EU imports from China, but this could potentially mark the start of a broader shift
toward more trade protection in Europe with its largest foreign supplier which risks adding
further upward pressure to cost-push inflation. And though the leading candidates in the
French election may favor resuming oil and gas imports from Russia, Europe also remains

among the most at risk from the global energy shock. Indeed some 56% of the region’s
crude oil consumption is in the form of diesel product according to the International Energy
Agency, more than twice the U.S. share.
The 2- to 10-year eurozone yield curve has already flattened significantly since the start
of the year. And at just over 30 basis points as of mid-October, it remains flatter than
its equivalents in the U.S., UK and Japan. Outside Energy and Technology, Financials have

been among the strongest-performing sectors in Europe this year. But additional ECB
rate hikes could lead to further curve flattening, compressing bank net interest margins
and contributing to slower growth—particularly given that rate increases are coming in
response to supply-driven inflation rather than reflecting stronger economic activity.
We nonetheless continue to see pockets of strength in European markets. Growing
defense budgets should remain a tailwind for Industrials as national governments look to

meet their North Atlantic Treaty Organization targets over the next several years. And the
sector should be further supported by indirect exposure to AI investment through electric
cabling, power infrastructure and building materials for data centers. Leaders in renewable
energy equipment and services are also likely to be beneficiaries of grid upgrades and
broadening electrification globally, particularly given elevated fossil fuel prices. But our view

on the region remains cautious overall. On top of growing uncertainty from the upcoming
electoral cycle and interest rate outlook, rising competition and slowing demand from
China continue to challenge key segments of Europe’s Consumer Discretionary sector.
Limited direct AI concentration in technology hardware, cloud services and application
software also leaves Europe underexposed to the key growth theme that continues to buoy
equity markets in the U.S., Japan and emerging Asia.

### Page 6

6 of 8 October 19, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
THOUGHT OF THE WEEK
Cautious Optimism Entering Fiscal Year 2027
Ariana Chiu, Assistant Vice President and Investment Strategist
“A renewed focus on rebuilding fiscal space is urgent, given elevated debt, higher
borrowing costs, multiplying demands on public budgets and heightened uncertainty.” So
warned the IMF about the global fiscal outlook last week in its updated World Economic

Outlook report. Much is at stake for developed nations in Europe and Asia facing high
existing debt, unfavorable demographics and more modest growth.
For the U.S., between structurally higher mandatory outlays, the administration’s $1.5T
proposed defense package, and the 10-year U.S. Treasury yield sitting above 5%, spending
is likely headed higher. We continue to fade concerns of an impending U.S. debt crisis,
however, largely because economic growth continues to surprise to the upside. Expanding

output is good news for the debt-to-GDP ratio, which remains a touch below 100%.
Indeed, rather than being led higher on account of un-anchored inflation expectations, the
10-year U.S. Treasury yield has repriced to reflect better-than-expected nominal growth
(Exhibit 5A). Higher yields in the face of slowing growth, meanwhile, would be a different
story, not only for the fiscal outlook (worse) but also for U.S. equity valuations (lower).
Of course, after the U.S. accumulated another $2T deficit in FY 2026, 2 “rebuilding fiscal

space” in the IMF’s words will be politically unpopular and mathematically challenging.
In our view, the biggest near-term tail risk would be a surprise move lower in growth as
budget deficits at 6% of GDP in good times leave little room for bad times. This isn’t our
base case given robust consumer spending per our internal credit and debit card data and
ongoing investment in the AI buildout and beyond. We expect these two pillars to sustain
growth in 2027.

The other good news is that, contrary to concerns, demand for Treasurys remains robust.
Yields are attractive—that was the message from October’s successful $39B 10-year
Treasury note auction. At the same time, U.S. debt boasts an array of buyers: domestic
households, the Federal Reserve, mutual funds and exchange-traded funds, money market
funds, pension funds, foreign central banks, private investors overseas, and more. Per
Exhibit 5B, whereas foreign official investors once dominated some 40% of the U.S.

Treasury market during the 2008/2009 Great Financial Crisis, their share has fallen to
12% while domestic private investors now account for a majority of holdings. Translated:
demand is more diverse than most appreciate.

Investment Implications
In our view, higher nominal and real
yields present a compelling entry point
for investors seeking income and can
provide cushion in the face of higher
rate volatility. We expect demand for
U.S. assets to remain healthy and favor
the U.S. over developed economies in
Europe facing more modest growth and
fiscal instability.

of America, Merrill or any affiliate to sell or provide, or a specific invitation for a consumer to apply for, any particular retail financial product or service that may be available.

Investments have varying degrees of risk. Some of the risks involved with equity securities include the possibility that the value of the stocks may fluctuate in response to events specific to

the companies or markets, as well as economic, political or social events in the U.S. or abroad. Small cap and mid cap companies pose special risks, including possible illiquidity and greater price
volatility than funds consisting of larger, more established companies. Investing in fixed-income securities may involve certain risks, including the credit quality of individual issuers, possible
prepayments, market or economic developments and yields and share price fluctuations due to changes in interest rates. When interest rates go up, bond prices typically drop, and vice versa.

Investments in high-yield bonds (sometimes referred to as “junk bonds”) offer the potential for high current income and attractive total return, but involves certain risks. Changes in economic
conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect a junk bond issuer’s ability to make principal and interest payments. Income from investing in municipal bonds is generally exempt
from Federal and state taxes for residents of the issuing state. While the interest income is tax-exempt, any capital gains distributed are taxable to the investor. Income for some investors

may be subject to the Federal Alternative Minimum Tax (AMT). Treasury bills are less volatile than longer-term fixed income securities and are guaranteed as to timely payment of principal and
interest by the U.S. government. Bonds are subject to interest rate, inflation and credit risks. Investments in foreign securities (including ADRs) involve special risks, including foreign currency
risk and the possibility of substantial volatility due to adverse political, economic or other developments. These risks are magnified for investments made in emerging markets. Investments in

a certain industry or sector may pose additional risk due to lack of diversification and sector concentration. There are special risks associated with an investment in commodities, such as gold,
including market price fluctuations, regulatory changes, interest rate changes, credit risk, economic changes and the impact of adverse political or financial factors.
Alternative investments are speculative and involve a high degree of risk.
Alternative investments are intended for qualified investors only. Alternative Investments such as derivatives, hedge funds, private credit, private equity funds, and funds of funds can result

in higher return potential but also higher loss potential. Changes in economic conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect your investments. Before you invest in alternative
investments, you should consider your overall financial situation, how much money you have to invest, your need for liquidity, and your tolerance for risk.

### Page 1

Jan Hatzius 
+1(212)902-0394 | jan.hatzius@gs.com 
Goldman Sachs & Co. LLC
Global Views: Downside Growth Risks
26 October 2026 | 6:45AM EDT
1. So far in 2026, global growth has been remarkably unremarkable.  Both the world 
as a whole and the G3 economies are growing in line with our estimates of potential 
GDP .  Our interpretation is that fiscal easing and the positive impulse from the AI 
boom have offset the negative impulse from the war in Iran and the lingering drag 
from higher US tariffs.

2. Our standing global GDP forecasts project more of the same in 2027, as lower 
energy prices offset fading tailwinds from fiscal policy and AI.  But the balance of 
risks is on the downside.  The most immediate concern is that, far from subsiding, 
energy prices have again climbed sharply on fears of renewed Middle East escalation.  
The prices of gasoline, diesel, jet fuel, and European/Asian natural gas are now near 
or even above their March/April highs, with likely negative effects on consumers and 
energy-importing economies more broadly.

### Page 2

3. Although we don’t expect significant fiscal policy changes in 2027, the consequences 
of large-scale deficits—coupled with AI-linked borrowing and central bank hikes—have 
started to show up more clearly in higher longer-term interest rates.  The moves have 
been mostly orderly, but the increase since early 2026 averages a sizable 100bp across 
DM yield curves.  If sustained, we estimate that this increase will subtract a cumulative 
0.6% from GDP via weakness in housing, consumer durables, and business investment.  
(Only part of the drag is incorporated in our standing forecasts because we expect bond 
yields to pull back as central banks deliver fewer hikes than priced in markets.)

Exhibit 2: Oil Price Risks Are Skewed to the Upside

Source: Goldman Sachs Global Investment Research
26 October 2026   2
Goldman Sachs Global Views

### Page 3

4. The growth impulse from the AI boom was set to peak in late 2026 or early 2027 even 
before concerns around the sustainability of the AI investment boom intensified in 
recent weeks.  Although AI investment is still rising rapidly, we expect the increase as a 
share of GDP to slow over the next year.  Alongside the fading boost from last year’s 
increase in US depreciation allowances, this will weigh on capital spending growth, 
especially in the US.  AI has also boosted US consumer spending growth via a positive 
wealth effect worth an estimated 0.4pp (or 0.25% of GDP) over the past year.  This 
impact is likely to decline over the next year, gradually if stocks perform in line with our 
strategists’ reasonably upbeat expectations but more sharply if AI-related stocks 
continue to decline.

### Page 4

5. Our own base case remains that AI investment will ultimately pay off in terms of higher 
productivity and economywide profits.  However, one key channel for this payoff is labor 
market disruption, which has negative near-term macroeconomic effects.  And while DM 
labor markets still look solid in aggregate, we are starting to see clearer signs that the AI 
impact is spreading beyond the technology sector.  Across nonmanufacturing industries, 
the share of US firms that have adopted AI in regular business functions now shows a 
statistically significant negative correlation with employment growth and a statistically 
significant positive correlation with unemployment.

6. The Fed’s recent signals—explicit from Williams/Jefferson and implicit from Warsh—
effectively rule out a hike on October 28.  But the FOMC still looks very likely to deliver 
another 25bp hike to 4-4.25% on December 9 because spot inflation remains too high.  
And while we continue to expect significant disinflation next year on the back of 
reduced impulses from tariffs, energy prices, and AI-related prices, the risks to our 
forecast are on the upside, most obviously in energy.  Higher inflation could result in

another hike in March, but either way we see renewed cuts in late 2027/early 2028.

7. Like the Fed, the ECB will likely hold rates steady next week and deliver a final hike (to 
2.75%) at the December meeting.  However, the turbulence in the French bond market 
poses somewhat greater downside risks to that call than in the US.  The turbulence has 
tightened financial conditions to a degree that is likely to shave ¼-½pp from annualized 
growth in coming quarters.  And if it intensifies into December, markets might perceive a 
hike as signaling insufficient focus by the ECB on the risk of Euro area fragmentation.  
While the ECB could address this issue by coupling a hike with renewed balance sheet 
expansion, the hurdle for such a move is high, in part because purchases of French 
bonds would inevitably raise concerns about moral hazard with respect to the needed 
fiscal adjustment.

### Page 6

November 5.  Consistent with our view that the UK economy is stronger than widely 
perceived, GDP growth has surprised on the upside this year and the unemployment rate 
declined to 4.7% in August on a 3-month average basis (with the volatile 1-month rate 
plunging to 4.1%).  Rising inflation and the looming household energy price reset in 
January imply a significant risk of further moves, although market pricing of 4+ hikes 
(including November) still looks excessive.  We also expect hikes in Canada and New 
Zealand this week and in Australia next week.

9. Slightly faster than expected growth in Q3 has pushed up our 2026 GDP forecast for 
China to 4.6%.  But most activity indicators are much weaker.  Both property activity and 
fixed asset investment are contracting year-on-year, and while nominal retail sales 
growth picked up to +1.1% in September, large retailers (whose sales are relatively 
better measured) are still posting substantial year-on-year declines.  With domestic 
demand rising slowly at best, sustained GDP growth in the official 4.5-5% target range 
will require ongoing steep increases in the trade surplus.  But the surplus is already at 1% 
of global GDP , and further increases will eat more noticeably into output and 
employment abroad.  Ultimately, China’s trading partners may respond by imposing 
trade barriers that weigh on Chinese exports and GDP , although the measures taken so 
far have been limited.

Exhibit 8: UK Growth Forecasts Have Drifted Higher

Source: Bloomberg, Goldman Sachs Global Investment Research
26 October 2026   6
Goldman Sachs Global Views

### Page 7

10. Our strongest global view is that central banks will deliver fewer hikes than currently 
priced.  In addition, our rates strategists flag underweight positioning, diminished 
technical risks, and risk asset pressures as reasons why the recent rally might extend.  
The market’s narrow breadth makes our equity strategists wary of momentum factor 
exposure, but they still expect the S&P 500 to resume its climb after the midterm 
election, as earnings continue to grow solidly.  Our credit strategists remain cautious on 
IG technology bonds but see scope for some near-term reversal in HY given the scale of 
the recent selloff.  Our FX strategists expect the Japanese yen to act as a hedge against 
further AI jitters, in part because such jitters would shrink US-Japan interest rate 
differentials.  And our commodity strategists recommend targeted exposures to oil 
products and European natural gas to hedge near-term escalation risks alongside 
longer-term exposures to copper, gold, and select power markets to benefit from 
structural supply/demand imbalances. 
Jan Hatzius

### Page 1

Abhay Duggirala 
+1(202)585-6760 | 
abhay.duggirala@gs.com 
Goldman Sachs & Co. LLC
US Daily: Why Has the US Economy Been So Resilient to Higher Rates 
and Higher Oil Prices?
28 September 2026 | 10:38AM EDT
We estimate that the economy grew at a 3.4% pace in Q3 and forecast 2.2% n
growth in 2026 overall on a Q4/Q4 basis, close to our estimate of potential GDP 
growth and only 0.3pp below our start-of-the-year forecast before the large 
increases in interest rates and oil prices. Why, we are often asked, has the US 
economy proven so resilient to these shocks? 
While interest rates have risen substantially this year, our financial conditions n
index framework implies that the economic impact should be nearly offset by the 
effects of higher equity prices, narrower credit spreads, and a weaker dollar, 
resulting in only a 0.1pp net hit to 2026 GDP growth. While the net impact of this 
year’s market moves on overall GDP is modest, the effects on some parts of the 
economy—especially the hit to housing—are more meaningful. 
We estimate that the increase in oil and refined product prices this year, together n
with the path our commodities strategists expect through year-end, will subtract 
0.3pp from 2026 growth. The drag comes from consumption as higher energy 
prices erode real purchasing power. While US energy producers were reluctant to 
boost investment when oil prices first rose, the increase in the rig count index in 
recent months points to some offsetting boost to GDP in Q3 and Q4. 
Our analysis suggests that slightly tighter financial conditions and higher oil n
prices should together be roughly a 0.4pp drag on GDP growth. The downgrade 
we have made to our forecast for the year, in part based on incoming data, is only 
slightly smaller than that, with the slight outperformance coming largely from 
consumer spending. This suggests that the economy’s resilience is less puzzling 
than it might appear at first.

### Page 2

Why Has the US Economy Been So Resilient to Higher Rates and Higher Oil Prices?

We now estimate that the economy grew at a 3.4% pace in Q3 and forecast 2.2% growth 
in 2026 overall on a Q4/Q4 basis, close to our 2.3% estimate of potential GDP growth 
and only 0.3pp below our start-of-the-year forecast when we did not expect large 
increases in interest rates and oil prices. In today’s Daily, we ask why the US economy has 
proven so resilient to these surprises. 
Part of the answer is that financial conditions have changed little on net since January, as 
the substantial rise in interest rates has been nearly offset by the effects of higher equity 
prices, narrower credit spreads, and a weaker dollar (Exhibit 1, left). Our estimated GDP 
growth impulse from the FCI is therefore little changed from the start of the year (Exhibit 
1, right), implying only a 0.1pp hit to 2026 Q4/Q4 GDP growth.

While the net impact of this year’s market moves on overall GDP is modest, the effects 
on some components of GDP are more meaningful. We use the Fed’s macroeconomic 
model FRB/US to estimate how the particular combination of market moves this year 
should affect each component of GDP . We find a meaningful hit from higher rates to 
residential investment, offset by slight boosts to consumption and business investment 
(Exhibit 2).

Dollar

### Page 3

What about the effect of higher oil and refined products prices? Our rule of thumb 
implies that a sustained $10/barrel increase in oil prices lowers GDP growth by just over 
0.1pp through the consumption channel as higher energy costs erode real purchasing 
power. Applied to the path oil prices have taken this year, the unusual widening in 
refined product spreads, and our commodities strategists’ forecasts for prices through 
year-end, the rule of thumb implies a 0.4pp drag on GDP growth. 
While US oil producers were initially reluctant to respond to higher prices by increasing 
capex because of the uncertainty of the war with Iran and the outlook for oil prices, the 
increase in the rig count index in Q3 (Exhibit 3) points to a pickup in energy capex more 
recently. We estimate that the consumption drag and capex boost will net out to a 0.3pp 
hit to GDP growth from the oil shock. Two alternative approaches, FRB/US simulations 
and a model estimated using exogenous oil shocks, deliver similar estimates on average.1 
1 We use the series of exogenous oil shocks from Känzig (2021), “The Macroeconomic Effects of Oil Supply 
News: Evidence from OPEC Announcements” to estimate the effects of the current oil shock on GDP growth.

component under this year's changes in market conditions versus a counterfactual where
conditions were unchanged from the start of the year.

### Page 4

Our analysis suggests that slightly tighter financial conditions and higher oil prices 
should together be roughly a 0.4pp drag on GDP growth. The downgrade we have made 
to our forecast for the year, in part based on incoming data, is only slightly smaller than 
that, suggesting that the puzzle of economic resilience is smaller than it might appear at 
first. 
The economy’s slight outperformance relative to what we might have expected largely 
comes from consumer spending. At the start of the year, we forecasted consumption 
growth of 2.4% in 2026 Q4/Q4. Under the market and commodity price moves that 
actually materialized, our oil shock model implies a 0.6pp hit to that forecast, partially 
offset by a 0.2pp boost from higher equity prices in our wealth effects model, netting to 
an expected 2.0% growth in consumption this year. Against that, we now estimate—in 
part based on the surprisingly solid incoming data in recent months—that consumption 
will grow 2.2%, a modest upside surprise of 0.2pp.

Document 452: US Midterms_ The cost of living is now a pivotal issue (2026-11-02)

Source PDF: all report pdf/US Midterms_ The cost of living is now a pivotal issue (2026-11-02).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
